{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_vrtx_01Lne25qw77AnhugaTUHmwUG","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→# Simple Preference Optimization (SimPO)\n     2→\n     3→This repository contains the code and released models for our paper [SimPO: Simple Preference Optimization with a Reference-Free Reward](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.14734). We propose a simpler and more effective preference optimization algorithm than DPO (Direct Preference Optimization) without using a reference model. SimPO outperforms DPO and its latest variants across AlpacaEval 2, MT-Bench, and Arena-Hard benchmarks under various settings. Please find all the released model checkpoints at [this link](https://huggingface.co/collections/princeton-nlp/simpo-66500741a5a066eb7d445889). \n     4→\n     5→<img src=\"./SimPO.png\" width=\"1000px\"></img>\n     6→\n     7→## 🆕 Changelog \n     8→- **[2024.10.12]** To facilitate reproducibility, we release the training curves for Llama3-Instruct and Gemma2-IT:\n     9→  - [Llama3-Instruct-SimPO](https://wandb.ai/yumeng0818/simpo/runs/zoesxyuj)\n    10→  - [Llama3-Instruct-SimPO v0.2](https://wandb.ai/yumeng0818/simpo/runs/zvv56fcj)\n    11→  - [Gemma2-IT-SimPO](https://wandb.ai/yumeng0818/simpo/runs/4w25j650)\n    12→- **[2024.07.17]** We released a new SimPO model [gemma-2-9b-it-SimPO](https://huggingface.co/princeton-nlp/gemma-2-9b-it-SimPO) by fine-tuning Google's gemma-2 9B model using on-policy [UltraFeedback data](https://huggingface.co/datasets/princeton-nlp/gemma2-ultrafeedback-armorm) annotated by [ArmoRM](https://huggingface.co/RLHFlow/ArmoRM-Llama3-8B-v0.1), achieving a **72.4** LC win rate on AlpacaEval 2 (**#[1 on the Leaderboard](https://tatsu-lab.github.io/alpaca_eval/)** 🎉🎉) and a **59.1** win rate on Arena-Hard! Please find the training script [here](https://github.com/princeton-nlp/SimPO/blob/main/training_configs/gemma-2-9b-it-simpo.yaml) and the data generation scripts [here](https://github.com/princeton-nlp/SimPO/tree/main/on_policy_data_gen)!\n    13→- **[2024.07.08]** We updated our paper ([v2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.14734v2))\n    14→  - Additional baselines (RRHF, SLiC-HF, CPO) \n    15→  - New Llama3-Instruct setting (v0.2) with [ArmoRM](https://huggingface.co/RLHFlow/ArmoRM-Llama3-8B-v0.1) as the preference label annotator, yielding a better-performing model, [Llama-3-Instruct-8B-SimPO-v0.2](https://huggingface.co/princeton-nlp/Llama-3-Instruct-8B-SimPO-v0.2), with a **53.7** LC win rate on AlpacaEval 2 and a **36.5** win rate on Arena-Hard ([training script](https://github.com/princeton-nlp/SimPO/blob/main/training_configs/llama-3-8b-instruct-simpo-v2.yaml))!\n    16→  - [SimPO trainer](https://github.com/princeton-nlp/SimPO/blob/main/scripts/simpo_trainer.py) update for better reproducibility. The hyperparameter `gamma` changed to `gamma_beta_ratio` for easier tuning.\n    17→\n    18→## 🔗 Quick Links\n    19→- [SimPO: Simple Preference Optimization with a Reference-Free Reward](#simple-preference-optimization-simpo)\n    20→  - [Changelog](#-changelog)\n    21→  - [Tips for Running SimPO](#tips-for-running-simpo)\n    22→  - [Released Models](#released-models)\n    23→  - [Install Requirements](#install-requirements)\n    24→  - [Training scripts](#training-scripts)\n    25→  - [Evaluation](#evaluation)\n    26→  - [Bugs or Questions?](#bugs-or-questions)\n    27→  - [Citation](#citation)\n    28→\n    29→## Tips for Running SimPO\n    30→Given the various inquiries about SimPO, we provide a list of tips to help you reproduce our paper results and achieve better outcomes for running SimPO on your own tasks. \n    31→\n    32→### Environment\n    33→We provide an [environment file](https://github.com/princeton-nlp/SimPO/blob/main/environment.yml) including the python package versions we used in our experiments. For optimal reproducibility, we recommend using the same package versions. However, please note that results may still vary due to differences in hardware configurations and CUDA versions, etc.\n    34→\n    35→### Hyperparameter tuning\n    36→Hyperparameter tuning is crucial for SimPO (and other preference optimization algorithms in general). The three main hyperparameters of SimPO to focus on are `learning_rate`, `beta`, and `gamma` (we recommend keeping the total batch size fixed at 128).\n    37→- `learning_rate`: It is the most critical hyperparameter for preference optimization. A large learning rate (e.g., 1e-5) can significantly degrade performance, causing the model to produce incoherent sentences or completely repetitive responses. We recommend grid searching over 3e-7, 5e-7, 8e-7, and 1e-6, if resources allow. **We find that a smaller learning rate (e.g., 5e-7) is more suitable for reasoning intensive domains like math for both DPO and SimPO.**\n    38→- `beta`: Beta controls the reward scaling between winning and losing responses. SimPO requires a much larger `beta` than DPO. In our preprint, we used a beta of `2.0` or `2.5`, but in many cases, an even larger beta (e.g., `10`) could yield better results.\n    39→- `gamma`: Gamma controls the target reward margin. We suggest tuning the ratio of gamma to beta (i.e., `gamma / beta`). We recommend using `0.5` as a starting point for `gamma_beta_ratio` and grid searching between `0` and `1`. A well-tuned `gamma_beta_ratio` can provide a modest improvement, but it is not as critical as other hyperparameters.\n    40→\n    41→We used the following hyperparameters for training the released models (note that in our latest update, we changed the hyperparameter `gamma` to `gamma_beta_ratio` as the latter is normalized and easier to tune under different `beta` values).\n    42→| Setting           | β   | γ/β   | Learning rate |\n    43→|-------------------|-----|-----|----------------|\n    44→| Mistral-Base      | 2.0 | 0.8 | 3e-7           |\n    45→| Mistral-Instruct  | 2.5 | 0.1 | 5e-7           |\n    46→| Llama3-Base       | 2.0 | 0.5 | 6e-7           |\n    47→| Llama3-Instruct   | 2.5 | 0.55 | 1e-6           |\n    48→| Llama3-Instruct v0.2   | 10 | 0.3 | 1e-6           |\n    49→| Gemma             | 10 | 0.5 | 8e-7 |  \n    50→\n    51→For DPO, the best hyperparameters for each setting are as follows.\n    52→| Setting                  | β | Learning Rate |\n    53→|------------------------|------|---------------|\n    54→| Mistral-Base           | 0.01 | 5e-7      |\n    55→| Mistral-Instruct       | 0.01 | 5e-7      |\n    56→| Llama3-Base            | 0.01 | 5e-7      |\n    57→| Llama3-Instruct        | 0.01 | 7e-7      |\n    58→| Llama3-Instruct v0.2   | 0.01 | 3e-7      |\n    59→| Gemma             | 0.01 | 5e-7 |  \n    60→\n    61→\n    62→### Training and evaluation consistency in BOS\n    63→Our released Llama3 models use the initial version of the Llama3 tokenizer (prior to this [PR](https://huggingface.co/meta-llama/Meta-Llama-3-8B-Instruct/commit/339ce92d052f002cdbac4a4bd551d1c61dd8345e)). We have found that the updated Llama3 tokenizer with vLLM occasionally introduces two BOS tokens, which can affect evaluation results. Therefore, please ensure that only one BOS token is included in the prompt after applying the Llama3 chat template during any evaluation.\n    64→\n    65→*Notably, if you are training Llama3 and evaluating the trained models on AlpacaEval 2 and Arena-Hard using the templates provided in this repo, please make sure to use the pre-update Llama3 tokenizer (i.e., the one before the PR).*\n    66→\n    67→### Reproducing AlpacaEval 2 numbers\n    68→Please make sure that you use `alpaca-eval==0.6.2` and [model configurations](https://github.com/princeton-nlp/SimPO/tree/main/eval/alpacaeval2/configs) in our repo for successfully reproducing AlpacaEval 2 results. AlpacaEval has a major revision for vllm decoding since `0.6.3` and causes a discrepancy from our experiments. \n    69→\n    70→### Adding an extra SFT loss\n    71→The [CPO_SIMPO](https://github.com/fe1ixxu/CPO_SIMPO/tree/main) repository did preliminary experiments and observed that in some cases, adding an additional SFT loss can help improve results. In our own experiments, the SFT regularization helps preserve the reasoning ability (e.g., GSM8K) but degrades chat performance. If you'd like to apply SFT regularization, you can set `sft_weight` to be a positive value (by default it's 0).\n    72→\n    73→\n    74→## Released Models\n    75→\n    76→### Gemma  \n    77→We release the following two models that are built on top of the strong [google/gemma-2-9b-it](https://huggingface.co/google/gemma-2-9b-it) model by training DPO and SimPO on the on-policy dataset [princeton-nlp/gemma2-ultrafeedback-armorm](https://huggingface.co/datasets/princeton-nlp/gemma2-ultrafeedback-armorm). For GSM and MMLU, we use the [ZeroEval](https://github.com/yuchenlin/ZeroEval) repository which aims to evaluate instruction-tuned LLMs (i.e., chat models instead of base models) for their zero-shot performance on reasoning and knowledge heavy tasks. More results on [WildBench](https://huggingface.co/spaces/allenai/WildBench) are coming soon. \n    78→\n    79→|               models                    | AE2 LC | AE2 WR | AE2 Length |  AH  | AH Length |  GSM | GSM Length | MMLU | MMLU Length |\n    80→|-----------------------------------|:------:|:------:|:----------:|:----:|:---------:|:----:|:----------:|:----:|:-----------:|\n    81→|        [google/gemma-2-9b-it](https://huggingface.co/google/gemma-2-9b-it)       |  51.1  |  38.1  |    1571    | 40.8 |    545    | 87.4 |     395    | 72.7 |     515     |\n    82→|  [princeton-nlp/gemma-2-9b-it-DPO](https://huggingface.co/princeton-nlp/gemma-2-9b-it-DPO)  |  67.8  |  65.4  |    2016    | 58.9 |    717    | 88.5 |     392    | 72.2 |     624     |\n    83→| [princeton-nlp/gemma-2-9b-it-SimPO](https://huggingface.co/princeton-nlp/gemma-2-9b-it-SimPO) |  72.4  |  65.9  |    1833    | 59.1 |    693    | 88.0 |     341    | 72.2 |     441     |\n    84→\n    85→- Compared to the llama3 models, we found that the gemma models exhibit significantly less catastrophic forgetting on math tasks (e.g., GSM) and MMLU, despite the ultrafeedback dataset having limited math-related data. This demonstrates that the [google/gemma-2-9b-it](https://huggingface.co/google/gemma-2-9b-it) model is more suitable for continued preference optimization.\n    86→- SimPO and DPO perform comparably across all benchmarks, but SimPO is inherently simpler and less resource-intensive.\n    87→\n    88→\n    89→### v0.2\n    90→We found that using a strong reward model for annotating preference optimization datasets is crucial. In this iteration, we have reannotated the dataset [princeton-nlp/llama3-ultrafeedback-armorm](https://huggingface.co/datasets/princeton-nlp/llama3-ultrafeedback-armorm) using a more powerful reward model, [RLHFlow/ArmoRM-Llama3-8B-v0.1](https://huggingface.co/RLHFlow/ArmoRM-Llama3-8B-v0.1). As a result, the v0.2 models demonstrate significantly improved performance compared to the v0.1 models. \n    91→\n    92→**Caveat**: We have observed that the SimPO v0.2 model often struggles with generating outputs that require adherence to specific structures, such as json. This issue arises from a combination of factors: the llama3-instruct model's tendency to forget and the large learning rate (e.g., 1e-6) used during training, which causes deviation from the original model. To address this, we developed SimPO models based on the [google/gemma-2-9b-it](https://huggingface.co/google/gemma-2-9b-it). We found that changing the initial model significantly mitigates the forgetting issue and reduces the impact of the learning rate.\n    93→\n    94→| models                       | 154→Please refer to the [generate.py](generate.py) script for detailed instructions on loading the model with the appropriate chat template.\n   155→\n   156→## Install Requirements\n   157→\n   158→Our codebase is built upon the [alignment-handbook repo](https://github.com/huggingface/alignment-handbook). The following steps will guide you through the installation process.\n   159→\n   160→First, create a Python virtual environment using e.g. Conda:\n   161→```shell\n   162→conda create -n handbook python=3.10 && conda activate handbook\n   163→```\n   164→\n   165→Next, install PyTorch `v2.2.2`. Since this is hardware-dependent, we\n   166→direct you to the [PyTorch Installation Page](https://pytorch.org/get-started/locally/).\n   167→\n   168→You can then install the remaining package dependencies of [alignment-handbook](https://github.com/huggingface/alignment-handbook) as follows:\n   169→\n   170→```shell\n   171→git clone https://github.com/huggingface/alignment-handbook.git\n   172→cd ./alignment-handbook/\n   173→python -m pip install .\n   174→```\n   175→\n   176→You will also need Flash Attention 2 installed, which can be done by running:\n   177→\n   178→```shell\n   179→python -m pip install flash-attn --no-build-isolation\n   180→```\n   181→\n   182→## Training Scripts\n   183→\n   184→We provide four training config files for the four training setups reported in our paper. The training config is set for 4xH100 GPUs. You may need to adjust `num_processes` and `per_device_train_batch_size` based on your computation environment. \n   185→\n   186→* Mistral-Base:\n   187→```shell\n   188→ACCELERATE_LOG_LEVEL=info accelerate launch --config_file accelerate_configs/deepspeed_zero3.yaml scripts/run_simpo.py training_configs/mistral-7b-base-simpo.yaml\n   189→```\n   190→* Mistral-Instruct:\n   191→```shell\n   192→ACCELERATE_LOG_LEVEL=info accelerate launch --config_file accelerate_configs/deepspeed_zero3.yaml scripts/run_simpo.py training_configs/mistral-7b-instruct-simpo.yaml\n   193→```\n   194→* Llama3-Base:\n   195→```shell\n   196→ACCELERATE_LOG_LEVEL=info accelerate launch --config_file accelerate_configs/deepspeed_zero3.yaml scripts/run_simpo.py training_configs/llama-3-8b-base-simpo.yaml\n   197→```\n   198→* Llama3-Instruct:\n   199→```shell\n   200→ACCELERATE_LOG_LEVEL=info accelerate launch --config_file accelerate_configs/deepspeed_zero3.yaml scripts/run_simpo.py training_configs/llama-3-8b-instruct-simpo.yaml\n   201→```\n   202→* Llama3-Instruct v0.2:\n   203→```shell\n   204→ACCELERATE_LOG_LEVEL=info accelerate launch --config_file accelerate_configs/deepspeed_zero3.yaml scripts/run_simpo.py training_configs/llama-3-8b-instruct-simpo-v2.yaml\n   205→```\n   206→\n   207→## Evaluation\n   208→\n   209→We follow the official implementation for evaluation on AlpacaEval 2, Arena-Hard, and MT-Bench, as follows (more details can be found under [the eval directory](https://github.com/princeton-nlp/SimPO/tree/main/eval)):\n   210→\n   211→* AlpacaEval 2: Please refer to the [AlpacaEval repo](https://github.com/tatsu-lab/alpaca_eval) for evaluation.\n   212→\n   213→* Arena-Hard: Please refer to to the [Arena-Hard-Auto repo](https://github.com/lm-sys/arena-hard-auto) for evaluation.\n   214→\n   215→* MT-Bench: Please refer to the [FastChat repo](https://github.com/lm-sys/FastChat) for evaluation.\n   216→\n   217→## Bugs or Questions?\n   218→If you have any questions related to the code or the paper, feel free to email Yu We propose a simpler and more effective preference optimization algorithm than DPO (Direct Preference Optimization) without using a reference model. SimPO outperforms DPO and its latest variants across AlpacaEval 2, MT-Bench, and Arena-Hard benchmarks under various settings. Please find all the released model checkpoints at [this link](https://huggingface.co/collections/princeton-nlp/simpo-66500741a5a066eb7d445889). \n\n<img src=\"./SimPO.png\" width=\"1000px\"></img>\n\n## 🆕 Changelog \n- **[2024.10.12]** To facilitate reproducibility, we release the training curves for Llama3-Instruct and Gemma2-IT:\n  - [Llama3-Instruct-SimPO](https://wandb.ai/yumeng0818/simpo/runs/zoesxyuj)\n  - [Llama3-Instruct-SimPO v0.2](https://wandb.ai/yumeng0818/simpo/runs/zvv56fcj)\n  - [Gemma2-IT-SimPO](https://wandb.ai/yumeng0818/simpo/runs/4w25j650)\n- **[2024.07.17]** We released a new SimPO model [gemma-2-9b-it-SimPO](https://huggingface.co/princeton-nlp/gemma-2-9b-it-SimPO) by fine-tuning Google's gemma-2 9B model using on-policy [UltraFeedback data](https://huggingface.co/datasets/princeton-nlp/gemma2-ultrafeedback-armorm) annotated by [ArmoRM](https://huggingface.co/RLHFlow/ArmoRM-Llama3-8B-v0.1), achieving a **72.4** LC win rate on AlpacaEval 2 (**#[1 on the Leaderboard](https://tatsu-lab.github.io/alpaca_eval/)** 🎉🎉) and a **59.1** win rate on Arena-Hard! Please find the training script [here](https://github.com/princeton-nlp/SimPO/blob/main/training_configs/gemma-2-9b-it-simpo.yaml) and the data generation scripts [here](https://github.com/princeton-nlp/SimPO/tree/main/on_policy_data_gen)!\n- **[2024.07.08]** We updated our paper ([v2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.14734v2))\n  - Additional baselines (RRHF, SLiC-HF, CPO) \n  - New Llama3-Instruct setting (v0.2) with [ArmoRM](https://huggingface.co/RLHFlow/ArmoRM-Llama3-8B-v0.1) as the preference label annotator, yielding a better-performing model, [Llama-3-Instruct-8B-SimPO-v0.2](https://huggingface.co/princeton-nlp/Llama-3-Instruct-8B-SimPO-v0.2), with a **53.7** LC win rate on AlpacaEval 2 and a **36.5** win rate on Arena-Hard ([training script](https://github.com/princeton-nlp/SimPO/blob/main/training_configs/llama-3-8b-instruct-simpo-v2.yaml))!\n  - [SimPO trainer](https://github.com/princeton-nlp/SimPO/blob/main/scripts/simpo_trainer.py) update for better reproducibility. The hyperparameter `gamma` changed to `gamma_beta_ratio` for easier tuning.\n\n## 🔗 Quick Links\n- [SimPO: Simple Preference Optimization with a Reference-Free Reward](#simple-preference-optimization-simpo)\n  - [Changelog](#-changelog)\n  - [Tips for Running SimPO](#tips-for-running-simpo)\n  - [Released Models](#released-models)\n  - [Install Requirements](#install-requirements)\n  - [Training scripts](#training-scripts)\n  - [Evaluation](#evaluation)\n  - [Bugs or Questions?](#bugs-or-questions)\n  - [Citation](#citation)\n\n## Tips for Running SimPO\nGiven the various inquiries about SimPO, we provide a list of tips to help you reproduce our paper results and achieve better outcomes for running SimPO on your own tasks. \n\n### Environment\nWe provide an [environment file](https://github.com/princeton-nlp/SimPO/blob/main/environment.yml) including the python package versions we used in our experiments. For optimal reproducibility, we recommend using the same package versions. However, please note that results may still vary due to differences in hardware configurations and CUDA versions, etc.\n\n### Hyperparameter tuning\nHyperparameter tuning is crucial for SimPO (and other preference optimization algorithms in general). The three main hyperparameters of SimPO to focus on are `learning_rate`, `beta`, and `gamma` (we recommend keeping the total batch size fixed at 128).\n- `learning_rate`: It is the most critical hyperparameter for preference optimization. A large learning rate (e.g., 1e-5) can significantly degrade performance, causing the model to produce incoherent sentences or completely repetitive responses. We recommend grid searching over 3e-7, 5e-7, 8e-7, and 1e-6, if resources allow. **We find that a smaller learning rate (e.g., 5e-7) is more suitable for reasoning intensive domains like math for both DPO and SimPO.**\n- `beta`: Beta controls the reward scaling between winning and losing responses. SimPO requires a much larger `beta` than DPO. In our preprint, we used a beta of `2.0` or `2.5`, but in many cases, an even larger beta (e.g., `10`) could yield better results.\n- `gamma`: Gamma controls the target reward margin. We suggest tuning the ratio of gamma to beta (i.e., `gamma / beta`). We recommend using `0.5` as a starting point for `gamma_beta_ratio` and grid searching between `0` and `1`. A well-tuned `gamma_beta_ratio` can provide a modest improvement, but it is not as critical as other hyperparameters.\n\nWe used the following hyperparameters for training the released models (note that in our latest update, we changed the hyperparameter `gamma` to `gamma_beta_ratio` as the latter is normalized and easier to tune under different `beta` values).\n| Setting           | β   | γ/β   | Learning rate |\n|-------------------|-----|-----|----------------|\n| Mistral-Base      | 2.0 | 0.8 | 3e-7           |\n| Mistral-Instruct  | 2.5 | 0.1 | 5e-7           |\n| Llama3-Base       | 2.0 | 0.5 | 6e-7           |\n| Llama3-Instruct   | 2.5 | 0.55 | 1e-6           |\n| Llama3-Instruct v0.2   | 10 | 0.3 | 1e-6           |\n| Gemma             | 10 | 0.5 | 8e-7 |  \n\nFor DPO, the best hyperparameters for each setting are as follows.\n| Setting                  | β | Learning Rate |\n|------------------------|------|---------------|\n| Mistral-Base           | 0.01 | 5e-7      |\n| Mistral-Instruct       | 0.01 | 5e-7      |\n| Llama3-Base            | 0.01 | 5e-7      |\n| Llama3-Instruct        | 0.01 | 7e-7      |\n| Llama3-Instruct v0.2   | 0.01 | 3e-7      |\n| Gemma             | 0.01 | 5e-7 |  \n\n\n### Training and evaluation consistency in BOS\nOur released Llama3 models use the initial version of the Llama3 tokenizer (prior to this [PR](https://huggingface.co/meta-llama/Meta-Llama-3-8B-Instruct/commit/339ce92d052f002cdbac4a4bd551d1c61dd8345e)). We have found that the updated Llama3 tokenizer with vLLM occasionally introduces two BOS tokens, which can affect evaluation results. Therefore, please ensure that only one BOS token is included in the prompt after applying the Llama3 chat template during any evaluation.\n\n*Notably, if you are training Llama3 and evaluating the trained models on AlpacaEval 2 and Arena-Hard using the templates provided in this repo, please make sure to use the pre-update Llama3 tokenizer (i.e., the one before the PR).*\n\n### Reproducing AlpacaEval 2 numbers\nPlease make sure that you use `alpaca-eval==0.6.2` and [model configurations](https://github.com/princeton-nlp/SimPO/tree/main/eval/alpacaeval2/configs) in our repo for successfully reproducing AlpacaEval 2 results. AlpacaEval has a major revision for vllm decoding since `0.6.3` and causes a discrepancy from our experiments. \n\n### Adding an extra SFT loss\nThe [CPO_SIMPO](https://github.com/fe1ixxu/CPO_SIMPO/tree/main) repository did preliminary experiments and observed that in some cases, adding an additional SFT loss can help improve results. In our own experiments, the SFT regularization helps preserve the reasoning ability (e.g., GSM8K) but degrades chat performance. If you'd like to apply SFT regularization, you can set `sft_weight` to be a positive value (by default it's 0).\n\n\n## Released Models\n\n### Gemma  \nWe release the following two models that are built on top of the strong [google/gemma-2-9b-it](https://huggingface.co/google/gemma-2-9b-it) model by training DPO and SimPO on the on-policy dataset [princeton-nlp/gemma2-ultrafeedback-armorm](https://huggingface.co/datasets/princeton-nlp/gemma2-ultrafeedback-armorm). For GSM and MMLU, we use the [ZeroEval](https://github.com/yuchenlin/ZeroEval) repository which aims to evaluate instruction-tuned LLMs (i.e., chat models instead of base models) for their zero-shot performance on reasoning and knowledge heavy tasks. More results on [WildBench](https://huggingface.co/spaces/allenai/WildBench) are coming soon. \n\n|               models                    | AE2 LC | AE2 WR | AE2 Length |  AH  | AH Length |  GSM | GSM Length | MMLU | MMLU Length |\n|-----------------------------------|:------:|:------:|:----------:|:----:|:---------:|:----:|:----------:|:----:|:-----------:|\n|        [google/gemma-2-9b-it](https://huggingface.co/google/gemma-2-9b-it)       |  51.1  |  38.1  |    1571    | 40.8 |    545    | 87.4 |     395    | 72.7 |     515     |\n|  [princeton-nlp/gemma-2-9b-it-DPO](https://huggingface.co/princeton-nlp/gemma-2-9b-it-DPO)  |  67.8  |  65.4  |    2016    | 58.9 |    717    | 88.5 |     392    | 72.2 |     624     |\n| [princeton-nlp/gemma-2-9b-it-SimPO](https://huggingface.co/princeton-nlp/gemma-2-9b-it-SimPO) |  72.4  |  65.9  |    1833    | 59.1 |    693    | 88.0 |     341    | 72.2 |     441     |\n\n- Compared to the llama3 models, we found that the gemma models exhibit significantly less catastrophic forgetting on math tasks (e.g., GSM) and MMLU, despite the ultrafeedback dataset having limited math-related data. This demonstrates that the [google/gemma-2-9b-it](https://huggingface.co/google/gemma-2-9b-it) model is more suitable for continued preference optimization.\n- SimPO and DPO perform comparably across all benchmarks, but SimPO is inherently simpler and less resource-intensive.\n\n\n### v0.2\nWe found that using a strong reward model for annotating preference optimization datasets is crucial. In this iteration, we have reannotated the dataset [princeton-nlp/llama3-ultrafeedback-armorm](https://huggingface.co/datasets/princeton-nlp/llama3-ultrafeedback-armorm) using a more powerful reward model, [RLHFlow/ArmoRM-Llama3-8B-v0.1](https://huggingface.co/RLHFlow/ArmoRM-Llama3-8B-v0.1). As a result, the v0.2 models demonstrate significantly improved performance compared to the v0.1 models. \n\n**Caveat**: We have observed that the SimPO v0.2 model often struggles with generating outputs that require adherence to specific structures, such as json. This issue arises from a combination of factors: the llama3-instruct model's tendency to forget and the large learning rate (e.g., 1e-6) used during training, which causes deviation from the original model. To address this, we developed SimPO models based on the [google/gemma-2-9b-it](https://huggingface.co/google/gemma-2-9b-it). We found that changing the initial model significantly mitigates the forgetting issue and reduces the impact of the learning rate.\n\n| models                       | [generate.py](generate.py) script for detailed instructions on loading the model with the appropriate chat template.\n\n## Install Requirements\n\nOur codebase is built upon the [alignment-handbook repo](https://github.com/huggingface/alignment-handbook). The following steps will guide you through the installation process.\n\nFirst, create a Python virtual environment using e.g. Conda:\n```shell\nconda create -n handbook python=3.10 && conda activate handbook\n```\n\nNext, install PyTorch `v2.2.2`. Since this is hardware-dependent, we\ndirect you to the [PyTorch Installation Page](https://pytorch.org/get-started/locally/).\n\nYou can then install the remaining package dependencies of [alignment-handbook](https://github.com/huggingface/alignment-handbook) as follows:\n\n```shell\ngit clone https://github.com/huggingface/alignment-handbook.git\ncd ./alignment-handbook/\npython -m pip install .\n```\n\nYou will also need Flash Attention 2 installed, which can be done by running:\n\n```shell\npython -m pip install flash-attn --no-build-isolation\n```\n\n## Training Scripts\n\nWe provide four training config files for the four training setups reported in our paper. The training config is set for 4xH100 GPUs. You may need to adjust `num_processes` and `per_device_train_batch_size` based on your computation environment. \n\n* Mistral-Base:\n```shell\nACCELERATE_LOG_LEVEL=info accelerate launch --config_file accelerate_configs/deepspeed_zero3.yaml scripts/run_simpo.py training_configs/mistral-7b-base-simpo.yaml\n```\n* Mistral-Instruct:\n```shell\nACCELERATE_LOG_LEVEL=info accelerate launch --config_file accelerate_configs/deepspeed_zero3.yaml scripts/run_simpo.py training_configs/mistral-7b-instruct-simpo.yaml\n```\n* Llama3-Base:\n```shell\nACCELERATE_LOG_LEVEL=info accelerate launch --config_file accelerate_configs/deepspeed_zero3.yaml scripts/run_simpo.py training_configs/llama-3-8b-base-simpo.yaml\n```\n* Llama3-Instruct:\n```shell\nACCELERATE_LOG_LEVEL=info accelerate launch --config_file accelerate_configs/deepspeed_zero3.yaml scripts/run_simpo.py training_configs/llama-3-8b-instruct-simpo.yaml\n```\n* Llama3-Instruct v0.2:\n```shell\nACCELERATE_LOG_LEVEL=info accelerate launch --config_file accelerate_configs/deepspeed_zero3.yaml scripts/run_simpo.py training_configs/llama-3-8b-instruct-simpo-v2.yaml\n```\n\n## Evaluation\n\nWe follow the official implementation for evaluation on AlpacaEval 2, Arena-Hard, and MT-Bench, as follows (more details can be found under [the eval directory](https://github.com/princeton-nlp/SimPO/tree/main/eval)):\n\n* AlpacaEval 2: Please refer to the [AlpacaEval repo](https://github.com/tatsu-lab/alpaca_eval) for evaluation.\n\n* Arena-Hard: Please refer to to the [Arena-Hard-Auto repo](https://github.com/lm-sys/arena-hard-auto) for evaluation.\n\n* MT-Bench: Please refer to the [FastChat repo](https://github.com/lm-sys/FastChat) for evaluation.\n\n## Bugs or Questions?\nIf you have any questions related to the code or the paper, feel free to email Yu (yumeng5@virginia.edu). If you encounter any problems when using the code, or want to report a bug, feel free to open an issue! Consider upgrading `transformers`.\"\n   295→        #     )\n   296→\n   297→    def build_tokenized_answer(self, prompt, answer):\n   298→        \"\"\"\n   299→        Llama tokenizer does satisfy `enc(a + b) = enc(a) + enc(b)`.\n   300→        It does ensure `enc(a + b) = enc(a) + enc(a + b)[len(enc(a)):]`.\n   301→        Reference:\n   302→            https://github.com/EleutherAI/lm-evaluation-harness/pull/531#issuecomment-1595586257\n   303→        \"\"\"\n   304→\n   305→        full_tokenized = self.tokenizer(prompt + answer, add_special_tokens=False)\n   306→        prompt_input_ids = self.tokenizer(prompt, add_special_tokens=False)[\"input_ids\"]\n   307→\n   308→        answer_input_ids = full_tokenized[\"input_ids\"][len(prompt_input_ids) :]\n   309→        answer_attention_mask = full_tokenized[\"attention_mask\"][len(prompt_input_ids) :]\n   310→\n   311→        # Concat tokens to form `enc(a) + enc(a + b)[len(enc(a)):]`\n   312→        full_concat_input_ids = np.concatenate([prompt_input_ids, answer_input_ids])\n   313→\n   314→        # Prepare input tokens for token by token comparison\n   315→        full_input_ids = np.array(full_tokenized[\"input_ids\"])\n   316→\n   317→        if len(full_input_ids) != len(full_concat_input_ids):\n   318→            raise ValueError(\"Prompt input ids and answer input ids should have the same length.\")\n   319→\n   320→        # On some tokenizers, like Llama-2 tokenizer, there are occasions where tokens\n   321→        # can be merged together when tokenizing prompt+answer. This could result\n   322→        # on the last token from the prompt being different when tokenized on its own\n   323→        # vs when done as prompt+answer.\n   324→        response_token_ids_start_idx = len(prompt_input_ids)\n   325→\n   326→        # If tokenized prompt is different than both prompt+answer, then it means the\n   327→        # last token has changed due to merging.\n   328→        if prompt_input_ids != full_tokenized[\"input_ids\"][:response_token_ids_start_idx]:\n   329→            response_token_ids_start_idx -= 1\n   330→\n   331→        prompt_input_ids = full_tokenized[\"input_ids\"][:response_token_ids_start_idx]\n   332→        prompt_attention_mask = full_tokenized[\"attention_mask\"][:response_token_ids_start_idx]\n   333→\n   334→        if len(prompt_input_ids) != len(prompt_attention_mask):\n   335→            raise ValueError(\"Prompt input ids and attention mask should have the same length.\")\n   336→\n   337→        answer_input_ids = full_tokenized[\"input_ids\"][response_token_ids_start_idx:]\n   338→        answer_attention_mask = full_tokenized[\"attention_mask\"][response_token_ids_start_idx:]\n   339→\n   340→        return dict(\n   341→            prompt_input_ids=prompt_input_ids,\n   342→            prompt_attention_mask=prompt_attention_mask,\n   343→            input_ids=answer_input_ids,\n   344→            attention_mask=answer_attention_mask,\n   345→        )\n   346→\n   347→    def tokenize_row(self, feature, model: Optional[Union[PreTrainedModel, nn.Module]] = None) -> Dict:\n   348→        \"\"\"Tokenize a single row from a SimPO specific dataset.\n   349→\n   350→        At this stage, we don't convert to PyTorch tensors yet; we just handle the truncation\n   351→        in case the prompt + chosen or prompt + rejected responses is/are too long. First\n   352→            we truncate the prompt; if we're still too long, we truncate the chosen/rejected.\n   353→\n   354→        We also create the labels for the chosen/rejected responses, which are of length equal to\n   355→            the sum of the length of the prompt and the chosen/rejected response, with\n   356→            label_pad_token_id  for the prompt tokens.\n   357→        \"\"\"\n   358→        batch = {}\n   359→        prompt = feature[\"prompt\"]\n   360→        chosen = feature[\"chosen\"]\n   361→        rejected = feature[\"rejected\"]\n   362→\n   363→        if not self.is_encoder_decoder:\n   364→            # Check issues below for more details\n   365→            #  1. https://github.com/huggingface/trl/issues/907\n   366→            #  2. https://github.com/EleutherAI/lm-evaluation-harness/pull/531#issuecomment-1595586257\n   367→            #  3. https://github.com/LianjiaTech/BELLE/issues/337\n   368→\n   369→            if not isinstance(prompt, str):\n   370→                raise ValueError(f\"prompt should be an str but got {type(prompt)}\")\n   371→            prompt_tokens = self.tokenizer(prompt, add_special_tokens=False)\n   372→            prompt_tokens = {f\"prompt_{k}\": v for k, v in prompt_tokens.items()}\n   373→\n   374→            if not isinstance(chosen, str):\n   375→                raise ValueError(f\"chosen should be an str but got {type(chosen)}\")\n   376→            chosen_tokens = self.build_tokenized_answer(prompt, chosen)\n   377→\n   378→            if not isinstance(rejected, str):\n   379→                raise ValueError(f\"rejected should be an str but got {type(rejected)}\")\n   380→            rejected_tokens = self.build_tokenized_answer(prompt, rejected)\n   381→\n   382→            # Last prompt token might get merged by tokenizer and\n   383→            # it should not be included for generation if that happens\n   384→            prompt_len_input_ids = len(prompt_tokens[\"prompt_input_ids\"])\n   385→\n   386→            chosen_prompt_len_input_ids = len(chosen_tokens[\"prompt_input_ids\"])\n   387→            rejected_prompt_len_input_ids = len(rejected_tokens[\"prompt_input_ids\"])\n   388→            prompt_len_input_ids = min(chosen_prompt_len_input_ids, rejected_prompt_len_input_ids)\n   389→\n   390→            for k, v in prompt_tokens.items():\n   391→                prompt_tokens[k] = v[:prompt_len_input_ids]\n   392→\n   393→            # Make sure prompts only have one different token at most an\n   394→            # and length only differs by 1 at most\n   395→            num_diff_tokens = sum(\n   396→                [a != b for a, b in zip(chosen_tokens[\"prompt_input_ids\"], rejected_tokens[\"prompt_input_ids\"])]\n   397→            )\n   398→            num_diff_len = abs(chosen_prompt_len_input_ids - rejected_prompt_len_input_ids)\n   399→            if num_diff_tokens > 1 or num_diff_len > 1:\n   400→                raise ValueError(\n   401→                    \"Chosen and rejected prompt_input_ids might only differ on the \"\n   402→                    \"last token due to tokenizer merge ops.\"\n   403→                )\n   404→\n   405→            # add BOS token to head of prompt. Avoid adding if it's already there\n   406→            bos_token_id = self.tokenizer.bos_token_id\n   407→            if prompt_len_input_ids == 0 or bos_token_id != prompt_tokens[\"prompt_input_ids\"][0]:\n   408→                prompt_tokens[\"prompt_input_ids\"] = [bos_token_id] + prompt_tokens[\"prompt_input_ids\"]\n   409→                prompt_tokens[\"prompt_attention_mask\"] = [1] + prompt_tokens[\"prompt_attention_mask\"]\n   410→            if chosen_prompt_len_input_ids == 0 or bos_token_id != chosen_tokens[\"prompt_input_ids\"][0]:\n   411→                chosen_tokens[\"prompt_input_ids\"] = [bos_token_id] + chosen_tokens[\"prompt_input_ids\"]\n   412→                chosen_tokens[\"prompt_attention_mask\"] = [1] + chosen_tokens[\"prompt_attention_mask\"]\n   413→            if rejected_prompt_len_input_ids == 0 or bos_token_id != rejected_tokens[\"prompt_input_ids\"][0]:\n   414→                rejected_tokens[\"prompt_input_ids\"] = [bos_token_id] + rejected_tokens[\"prompt_input_ids\"]\n   415→                rejected_tokens[\"prompt_attention_mask\"] = [1] + rejected_tokens[\"prompt_attention_mask\"]\n   416→\n   417→            # add EOS token to end of answer. Avoid adding if it's already there\n   418→            eos_token_id = self.tokenizer.eos_token_id\n   419→            if len(chosen_tokens[\"input_ids\"]) == 0 or eos_token_id != chosen_tokens[\"input_ids\"][-1]:\n   420→                chosen_tokens[\"input_ids\"].append(eos_token_id)\n   421→                chosen_tokens[\"attention_mask\"].append(1)\n   422→            if len(rejected_tokens[\"input_ids\"]) == 0 or eos_token_id != rejected_tokens[\"input_ids\"][-1]:\n   423→                rejected_tokens[\"input_ids\"].append(eos_token_id)\n   424→                rejected_tokens[\"attention_mask\"].append(1)\n   425→\n   426→            longer_response_length = max(len(chosen_tokens[\"input_ids\"]), len(rejected_tokens[\"input_ids\"]))\n   427→\n   428→            # if combined sequence is too long, truncate the prompt\n   429→            for answer_tokens in [chosen_tokens, rejected_tokens, prompt_tokens]:\n   430→                if len(answer_tokens[\"prompt_input_ids\"]) + longer_response_length > self.max_length:\n   431→                    if self.truncation_mode == \"keep_start\":\n   432→                        for k in [\"prompt_input_ids\", \"prompt_attention_mask\"]:\n   433→                            answer_tokens[k] = answer_tokens[k][: self.max_prompt_length]\n   434→                    elif self.truncation_mode == \"keep_end\":\n   435→                        for k in [\"prompt_input_ids\", \"prompt_attention_mask\"]:\n   436→                            answer_tokens[k] = answer_tokens[k][-self.max_prompt_length :]\n   437→                    else:\n   438→                        raise ValueError(f\"Unknown truncation mode: {self.truncation_mode}\")\n   439→\n   440→            # if that's still too long, truncate the response\n   441→            for answer_tokens in [chosen_tokens, rejected_tokens]:\n   442→                if len(answer_tokens[\"prompt_input_ids\"]) + longer_response_length > self.max_length:\n   443→                    for k in [\"input_ids\", \"attention_mask\"]:\n   444→                        answer_tokens[k] = answer_tokens[k][: self.max_length - self.max_prompt_length]\n   445→\n   446→            # Create labels\n   447→            chosen_sequence_tokens = {\n   448→                k: chosen_tokens[f\"prompt_{k}\"] + chosen_tokens[k] for k in [\"input_ids\", \"attention_mask\"]\n   449→            }\n   450→            rejected_sequence_tokens = {\n   451→                k: rejected_tokens[f\"prompt_{k}\"] + rejected_tokens[k] for k in [\"input_ids\", \"attention_mask\"]\n   452→            }\n   453→            chosen_sequence_tokens[\"labels\"] = chosen_sequence_tokens[\"input_ids\"][:]\n   454→            chosen_sequence_tokens[\"labels\"][: len(chosen_tokens[\"prompt_input_ids\"])] = [\n   455→                self.label_pad_token_id\n   456→            ] * len(chosen_tokens[\"prompt_input_ids\"])\n   457→            rejected_sequence_tokens[\"labels\"] = rejected_sequence_tokens[\"input_ids\"][:]\n   458→            rejected_sequence_tokens[\"labels\"][: len(rejected_tokens[\"prompt_input_ids\"])] = [\n   459→                self.label_pad_token_id\n   460→            ] * len(rejected_tokens[\"prompt_input_ids\"])\n   461→\n   462→            for k, toks in {\n   463→                \"chosen_\": chosen_sequence_tokens,\n   464→                \"rejected_\": rejected_sequence_tokens,\n   465→                \"\": prompt_tokens,\n   466→            }.items():\n   467→                for type_key, tokens in toks.items():\n   468→                    if type_key == \"token_type_ids\":\n   469→                        continue\n   470→                    batch[f\"{k}{type_key}\"] = tokens\n   471→\n   472→        else:\n   473→            chosen_tokens = self.tokenizer(\n   474→                chosen, truncation=True, max_length=self.max_target_length, add_special_tokens=True\n   475→            )\n   476→            rejected_tokens = self.tokenizer(\n   477→                rejected, truncation=True, max_length=self.max_target_length, add_special_tokens=True\n   478→            )\n   479→            prompt_tokens = self.tokenizer(\n   480→                prompt, truncation=True, max_length=self.max_prompt_length, add_special_tokens=True\n   481→            )\n   482→\n   483→            batch[\"chosen_labels\"] = chosen_tokens[\"input_ids\"]\n   484→            batch[\"rejected_labels\"] = rejected_tokens[\"input_ids\"]\n   485→            batch[\"prompt_input_ids\"] = prompt_tokens[\"input_ids\"]\n   486→            batch[\"prompt_attention_mask\"] = prompt_tokens[\"attention_mask\"]\n   487→\n   488→            if model is not None and hasattr(model, \"prepare_decoder_input_ids_from_labels\"):\n   489→                batch[\"rejected_decoder_input_ids\"] = model.prepare_decoder_input_ids_from_labels(\n   490→                    labels=torch.tensor(batch[\"rejected_labels\"])\n   491→                )\n   492→                batch[\"chosen_decoder_input_ids\"] = model.prepare_decoder_input_ids_from_labels(\n   493→                    labels=torch.tensor(batch[\"chosen_labels\"])\n   494→                )\n   495→\n   496→        return batch\n   497→\n   498→    @staticmethod\n   499→    def concatenated_inputs(\n   500→        batch: Dict[str, Union[List, torch.LongTensor]],\n   501→        is_encoder_decoder: bool = False,\n   502→        label_pad_token_id: int = -100,\n   503→        padding_value: int = 0,\n   504→        device: Optional[torch.device] = None,\n   505→    ) -> Dict[str, torch.LongTensor]:\n   506→        \"\"\"Concatenate the chosen and rejected inputs into a single tensor.\n   507→\n   508→        Args:\n   509→            batch: A batch of data. Must contain the keys 'chosen_input_ids' and 'rejected_input_ids', which are tensors of shape (batch_size, sequence_length).\n   510→            is_encoder_decoder: Whether the model is an encoder-decoder model.\n   511→            label_pad_token_id: The label pad token id.\n   512→            padding_value: The padding value to use for the concatenated inputs_ids.\n   513→            device: The device for the concatenated inputs.\n   514→\n   515→        Returns:\n   516→            A dictionary containing the concatenated inputs under the key 'concatenated_input_ids'.\n   517→        \"\"\"\n   518→        concatenated_batch = {}\n   519→\n   520→        if is_encoder_decoder:\n   521→            max_length = max(batch[\"chosen_labels\"].shape[1], batch[\"rejected_labels\"].shape[1])\n   522→        else:\n   523→            max_length = max(batch[\"chosen_input_ids\"].shape[1], batch[\"rejected_input_ids\"].shape[1])\n   524→\n   525→        for k in batch:\n   526→            if k.startswith(\"chosen\") and isinstance(batch[k], torch.Tensor):\n   527→                if \"labels\" in k or is_encoder_decoder:\n   528→                    pad_value = label_pad_token_id\n   529→                elif k.endswith(\"_input_ids\"):\n   530→                    pad_value = padding_value\n   531→                elif k.endswith(\"_attention_mask\"):\n   532→                    pad_value = 0\n   533→                concatenated_key = k.replace(\"chosen\", \"concatenated\")\n   534→                concatenated_batch[concatenated_key] = pad_to_length(batch[k], max_length, pad_value=pad_value)\n   535→        for k in batch:\n   536→            if k.startswith(\"rejected\") and isinstance(batch[k], torch.Tensor):\n   537→                if \"labels\" in k or is_encoder_decoder:\n   538→                    pad_value = label_pad_token_id\n   539→                elif k.endswith(\"_input_ids\"):\n   540→                    pad_value = padding_value\n   541→                elif k.endswith(\"_attention_mask\"):\n   542→                    pad_value = 0\n   543→                concatenated_key = k.replace(\"rejected\", \"concatenated\")\n   544→                concatenated_batch[concatenated_key] = torch.cat(\n   545→                    (\n   546→                        concatenated_batch[concatenated_key],\n   547→                        pad_to_length(batch[k], max_length, pad_value=pad_value),\n   548→                    ),\n   549→                    dim=0,\n   550→                ).to(device=device)\n   551→\n   552→        if is_encoder_decoder:\n   553→            concatenated_batch[\"concatenated_input_ids\"] = batch[\"prompt_input_ids\"].repeat(2, 1).to(device=device)\n   554→            concatenated_batch[\"concatenated_attention_mask\"] = (\n   555→                batch[\"prompt_attention_mask\"].repeat(2, 1).to(device=device)\n   556→            )\n   557→\n   558→        return concatenated_batch\n   559→\n   560→    def simpo_loss(\n   561→        self,\n   562→        policy_chosen_logps: torch.FloatTensor,\n   563→        policy_rejected_logps: torch.FloatTensor,\n   564→    ) -> Tuple[torch.FloatTensor, torch.FloatTensor, torch.FloatTensor]:\n   565→        \"\"\"Compute the SimPO loss for a batch of policy model log probabilities.\n   566→\n   567→        Args:\n   568→            policy_chosen_logps: Log probabilities of the policy model for the chosen responses. Shape: (batch_size,)\n   569→            policy_rejected_logps: Log probabilities of the policy model for the rejected responses. Shape: (batch_size,)\n   570→\n   571→        Returns:\n   572→            A tuple of three tensors: (losses, chosen_rewards, rejected_rewards).\n   573→            The losses tensor contains the SimPO loss for each example in the batch.\n   574→            The chosen_rewards and rejected_rewards tensors contain the rewards for the chosen and rejected responses, respectively.\n   575→        \"\"\"\n   576→\n   577→        return losses, chosen_rewards, rejected_rewards\n   578→\n   579→    def concatenated_forward(\n   580→        self, model: nn.Module, batch: Dict[str, Union[List, torch.LongTensor]]\n   581→    ) -> Tuple[torch.FloatTensor, torch.FloatTensor, torch.FloatTensor, torch.FloatTensor]:\n   582→        \"\"\"Run the given model on the given batch of inputs, concatenating the chosen and rejected inputs together.\n   583→\n   584→        We do this to avoid doing two forward passes, because it's faster for FSDP.\n   585→        \"\"\"\n   586→        concatenated_batch = self.concatenated_inputs(\n   587→            batch,\n   588→            is_encoder_decoder=self.is_encoder_decoder,\n   589→            label_pad_token_id=self.label_pad_token_id,\n   590→            padding_value=self.padding_value,\n   591→            device=self.accelerator.device,\n   592→        )\n   593→        len_chosen = batch[\"chosen_labels\"].shape[0]\n   594→\n   595→        model_kwargs = (\n   596→            {\n   597→                \"labels\": concatenated_batch[\"concatenated_labels\"],\n   598→                \"decoder_input_ids\": concatenated_batch.pop(\"concatenated_decoder_input_ids\", None),\n   599→            }\n   600→            if self.is_encoder_decoder\n   601→            else {}\n   602→        )\n   603→\n   604→        all_logits = model(\n   605→            concatenated_batch[\"concatenated_input_ids\"],\n   606→            attention_mask=concatenated_batch[\"concatenated_attention_mask\"],\n   607→            use_cache=False,\n   608→            **model_kwargs,\n   609→        ).logits\n   610→\n   611→        all_logps = self.get_batch_logps(\n   612→            all_logits,\n   613→            concatenated_batch[\"concatenated_labels\"],\n   614→            average_log_prob=True,\n   615→            is_encoder_decoder=self.is_encoder_decoder,\n   616→            label_pad_token_id=self.label_pad_token_id,\n   617→        )\n   618→\n   619→        chosen_logps = all_logps[:len_chosen]\n   620→        rejected_logps = all_logps[len_chosen:]\n   621→\n   622→        chosen_logits = all_logits[:len_chosen]\n   623→        rejected_logits = all_logits[len_chosen:]\n   624→\n   625→        chosen_labels = concatenated_batch[\"concatenated_labels\"][:len_chosen]\n   626→\n   627→        return (chosen_logps, rejected_logps, chosen_logits, rejected_logits, chosen_labels)\n   628→\n   629→    @staticmethod\n   630→    def get_batch_logps(\n   631→        logits: torch.FloatTensor,\n   632→        labels: torch.LongTensor,\n   633→        average_log_prob: bool = True,\n   634→        label_pad_token_id: int = -100,\n   635→        is_encoder_decoder: bool = False,\n   636→    ) -> torch.FloatTensor:\n   637→        \"\"\"Compute the log probabilities of the given labels under the given logits.\n   638→\n   639→        Args:\n   640→            logits: Logits of the model (unnormalized). Shape: (batch_size, sequence_length, vocab_size)\n   641→            labels: Labels for which to compute the log probabilities. Label tokens with a value of label_pad_token_id are ignored. Shape: (batch_size, sequence_length)\n   642→            average_log_prob: If True, return the average log probability per (non-masked) token. Otherwise, return the sum of the log probabilities of the (non-masked) tokens.\n   643→            label_pad_token_id: The label pad token id.\n   644→            is_encoder_decoder: Whether the model is an encoder-decoder model.\n   645→\n   646→        Returns:\n   647→            A tensor of shape (batch_size,) containing the average/sum log probabilities of the given labels under the given logits.\n   648→        \"\"\"\n   649→        if logits.shape[:-1] != labels.shape:\n   650→            raise ValueError(\"Logits (batch and sequence length dim) and labels must have the same shape.\")\n   651→\n   652→        if not is_encoder_decoder:\n   653→            labels = labels[:, 1:].clone()\n   654→            logits = logits[:, :-1, :]\n   655→        loss_mask = labels != label_pad_token_id\n   656→\n   657→        # dummy token; we'll ignore the losses on these tokens later\n   658→        labels[labels == label_pad_token_id] = 0\n   659→\n   660→        per_token_logps = torch.gather(logits.log_softmax(-1), dim=2, index=labels.unsqueeze(2)).squeeze(2)\n   661→\n   662→        if average_log_prob:\n   663→            return (per_token_logps * loss_mask).sum(-1) / loss_mask.sum(-1)\n   664→        else:\n   665→            return (per_token_logps * loss_mask).sum(-1)\n   666→\n   667→    def get_batch_loss_metrics(\n   668→        self,\n   669→        model,\n   670→        batch: Dict[str, Union[List, torch.LongTensor]],\n   671→        train_eval: Literal[\"train\", \"eval\"] = \"train\",\n   672→    ):\n   673→        \"\"\"Compute the SimPO loss and other metrics for the given batch of inputs for train or test.\"\"\"\n   674→        metrics = {}\n   675→        prefix = \"eval_\" if train_eval == \"eval\" else \"\"\n   676→\n   677→        (\n   678→            policy_chosen_logps,\n   679→            policy_rejected_logps,\n   680→            policy_chosen_logits,\n   681→            policy_rejected_logits,\n   682→            chosen_labels,\n   683→        ) = self.concatenated_forward(model, batch)\n   684→\n   685→        losses, chosen_rewards, rejected_rewards = self.simpo_loss(\n   686→            policy_chosen_logps,\n   687→            policy_rejected_logps,\n   688→        )\n   689→\n   690→        loss = losses.mean()\n   691→\n   692→        if self.sft_weight > 0.0:\n   693→            if not self.is_encoder_decoder:\n   694→                policy_chosen_logits = policy_chosen_logits[..., :-1, :].contiguous()\n   695→                chosen_labels = chosen_labels[..., 1:].clone()\n   696→            loss_func = nn.CrossEntropyLoss()\n   697→            sft_loss = loss_func(policy_chosen_logits.view(-1, policy_chosen_logits.shape[-1]), chosen_labels.view(-1))\n   698→            loss = self.sft_weight * sft_loss + loss\n   699→            metrics[f\"{prefix}sft_loss\"] = sft_loss.detach().cpu()\n   700→        \n   701→        reward_accuracies = (chosen_rewards > rejected_rewards).float()\n   702→\n   703→        metrics[f\"{prefix}rewards/chosen\"] = chosen_rewards.mean().cpu()\n   704→        metrics[f\"{prefix}rewards/rejected\"] = rejected_rewards.mean().cpu()\n   705→        metrics[f\"{prefix}rewards/accuracies\"] = reward_accuracies.mean().cpu()\n   706→        metrics[f\"{prefix}rewards/margins\"] = (chosen_rewards - rejected_rewards).mean().cpu()\n   707→        metrics[f\"{prefix}logps/rejected\"] = policy_rejected_logps.detach().mean().cpu()\n   708→        metrics[f\"{prefix}logps/chosen\"] = policy_chosen_logps.detach().mean().cpu()\n   709→        metrics[f\"{prefix}logits/rejected\"] = policy_rejected_logits.detach().mean().cpu()\n   710→        metrics[f\"{prefix}logits/chosen\"] = policy_chosen_logits.detach().mean().cpu()\n   711→\n   712→        return loss, metrics\n   713→\n   714→    def compute_loss(\n   715→        self,\n   716→        model: Union[PreTrainedModel, nn.Module],\n   717→        inputs: Dict[str, Union[torch.Tensor, Any]],\n   718→        return_outputs=False,\n   719→    ) -> Union[torch.Tensor, Tuple[torch.Tensor, Dict[str, torch.Tensor]]]:\n   720→        if not self.use_dpo_data_collator:\n   721→            warnings.warn(\n   722→                \"compute_loss is only implemented for DPODataCollatorWithPadding, and you passed a datacollator that is different than \"\n   723→                \"DPODataCollatorWithPadding - you might see unexpected behavior. Alternatively, you can implement your own prediction_step method if you are using a custom data collator\"\n   724→            )\n   725→\n   726→        compute_loss_context_manager = torch.cuda.amp.autocast if self._peft_has_been_casted_to_bf16 else nullcontext\n   727→\n   728→        with compute_loss_context_manager():\n   729→            loss, metrics = self.get_batch_loss_metrics(model, inputs, train_eval=\"train\")\n   730→\n   731→        # force log the metrics\n   732→        self.store_metrics(metrics, train_eval=\"train\")\n   733→\n   734→        if return_outputs:\n   735→            return (loss, metrics)\n   736→        return loss\n   737→\n   738→    def get_batch_samples(self, model, batch: Dict[str, torch.LongTensor]) -> Tuple[str, str]:\n   739→        \"\"\"Generate samples from the model and reference model for the given batch of inputs.\"\"\"\n   740→\n   741→        # If one uses `generate_during_eval` with peft + bf16, we need to explicitly call generate with\n   742→        # the torch cuda amp context manager as some hidden states are silently casted to full precision.\n   743→        generate_context_manager = nullcontext if not self._peft_has_been_casted_to_bf16 else torch.cuda.amp.autocast\n   744→\n   745→        with generate_context_manager():\n   746→            policy_output = model.generate(\n   747→                input_ids=batch[\"prompt_input_ids\"],\n   748→                attention_mask=batch[\"prompt_attention_mask\"],\n   749→                max_length=self.max_length,\n   750→                do_sample=True,\n   751→                pad_token_id=self.tokenizer.pad_token_id,\n   752→            )\n   753→\n   754→        policy_output = pad_to_length(policy_output, self.max_length, self.tokenizer.pad_token_id)\n   755→        policy_output_decoded = self.tokenizer.batch_decode(policy_output, skip_special_tokens=True)\n   756→\n   757→        return policy_output_decoded\n   758→\n   759→    def prediction_step(\n   760→        self,\n   761→        model: Union[PreTrainedModel, nn.Module],\n   762→        inputs: Dict[str, Union[torch.Tensor, Any]],\n   763→        prediction_loss_only: bool,\n   764→        ignore_keys: Optional[List[str]] = None,\n   765→    ):\n   766→        if not self.use_dpo_data_collator:\n   767→            warnings.warn(\n   768→                \"prediction_step is only implemented for DPODataCollatorWithPadding, and you passed a datacollator that is different than \"\n   769→                \"DPODataCollatorWithPadding - you might see unexpected behavior. Alternatively, you can implement your own prediction_step method if you are using a custom data collator\"\n   770→            )\n   771→        if ignore_keys is None:\n   772→            if hasattr(model, \"config\"):\n   773→                ignore_keys = getattr(model.config, \"keys_to_ignore_at_inference\", [])\n   774→            else:\n   775→                ignore_keys = []\n   776→\n   777→        prediction_context_manager = torch.cuda.amp.autocast if self._peft_has_been_casted_to_bf16 else nullcontext\n   778→\n   779→        with torch.no_grad(), prediction_context_manager():\n   780→            loss, metrics = self.get_batch_loss_metrics(model, inputs, train_eval=\"eval\")\n   781→\n   782→        # force log the metrics\n   783→        self.store_metrics(metrics, train_eval=\"eval\")\n   784→\n   785→        if prediction_loss_only:\n   786→            return (loss.detach(), None, None)\n   787→\n   788→        # Consider upgrading `transformers`.\"\n        #     )\n\n    def build_tokenized_answer(self, prompt, answer):\n        \"\"\"\n        Llama tokenizer does satisfy `enc(a + b) = enc(a) + enc(b)`.\n        It does ensure `enc(a + b) = enc(a) + enc(a + b)[len(enc(a)):]`.\n        Reference:\n            https://github.com/EleutherAI/lm-evaluation-harness/pull/531#issuecomment-1595586257\n        \"\"\"\n\n        full_tokenized = self.tokenizer(prompt + answer, add_special_tokens=False)\n        prompt_input_ids = self.tokenizer(prompt, add_special_tokens=False)[\"input_ids\"]\n\n        answer_input_ids = full_tokenized[\"input_ids\"][len(prompt_input_ids) :]\n        answer_attention_mask = full_tokenized[\"attention_mask\"][len(prompt_input_ids) :]\n\n        # Concat tokens to form `enc(a) + enc(a + b)[len(enc(a)):]`\n        full_concat_input_ids = np.concatenate([prompt_input_ids, answer_input_ids])\n\n        # Prepare input tokens for token by token comparison\n        full_input_ids = np.array(full_tokenized[\"input_ids\"])\n\n        if len(full_input_ids) != len(full_concat_input_ids):\n            raise ValueError(\"Prompt input ids and answer input ids should have the same length.\")\n\n        # On some tokenizers, like Llama-2 tokenizer, there are occasions where tokens\n        # can be merged together when tokenizing prompt+answer. This could result\n        # on the last token from the prompt being different when tokenized on its own\n        # vs when done as prompt+answer.\n        response_token_ids_start_idx = len(prompt_input_ids)\n\n        # If tokenized prompt is different than both prompt+answer, then it means the\n        # last token has changed due to merging.\n        if prompt_input_ids != full_tokenized[\"input_ids\"][:response_token_ids_start_idx]:\n            response_token_ids_start_idx -= 1\n\n        prompt_input_ids = full_tokenized[\"input_ids\"][:response_token_ids_start_idx]\n        prompt_attention_mask = full_tokenized[\"attention_mask\"][:response_token_ids_start_idx]\n\n        if len(prompt_input_ids) != len(prompt_attention_mask):\n            raise ValueError(\"Prompt input ids and attention mask should have the same length.\")\n\n        answer_input_ids = full_tokenized[\"input_ids\"][response_token_ids_start_idx:]\n        answer_attention_mask = full_tokenized[\"attention_mask\"][response_token_ids_start_idx:]\n\n        return dict(\n            prompt_input_ids=prompt_input_ids,\n            prompt_attention_mask=prompt_attention_mask,\n            input_ids=answer_input_ids,\n            attention_mask=answer_attention_mask,\n        )\n\n    def tokenize_row(self, feature, model: Optional[Union[PreTrainedModel, nn.Module]] = None) -> Dict:\n        \"\"\"Tokenize a single row from a SimPO specific dataset.\n\n        At this stage, we don't convert to PyTorch tensors yet; we just handle the truncation\n        in case the prompt + chosen or prompt + rejected responses is/are too long. First\n            we truncate the prompt; if we're still too long, we truncate the chosen/rejected.\n\n        We also create the labels for the chosen/rejected responses, which are of length equal to\n            the sum of the length of the prompt and the chosen/rejected response, with\n            label_pad_token_id  for the prompt tokens.\n        \"\"\"\n        batch = {}\n        prompt = feature[\"prompt\"]\n        chosen = feature[\"chosen\"]\n        rejected = feature[\"rejected\"]\n\n        if not self.is_encoder_decoder:\n            # Check issues below for more details\n            #  1. https://github.com/huggingface/trl/issues/907\n            #  2. https://github.com/EleutherAI/lm-evaluation-harness/pull/531#issuecomment-1595586257\n            #  3. https://github.com/LianjiaTech/BELLE/issues/337\n\n            if not isinstance(prompt, str):\n                raise ValueError(f\"prompt should be an str but got {type(prompt)}\")\n            prompt_tokens = self.tokenizer(prompt, add_special_tokens=False)\n            prompt_tokens = {f\"prompt_{k}\": v for k, v in prompt_tokens.items()}\n\n            if not isinstance(chosen, str):\n                raise ValueError(f\"chosen should be an str but got {type(chosen)}\")\n            chosen_tokens = self.build_tokenized_answer(prompt, chosen)\n\n            if not isinstance(rejected, str):\n                raise ValueError(f\"rejected should be an str but got {type(rejected)}\")\n            rejected_tokens = self.build_tokenized_answer(prompt, rejected)\n\n            # Last prompt token might get merged by tokenizer and\n            # it should not be included for generation if that happens\n            prompt_len_input_ids = len(prompt_tokens[\"prompt_input_ids\"])\n\n            chosen_prompt_len_input_ids = len(chosen_tokens[\"prompt_input_ids\"])\n            rejected_prompt_len_input_ids = len(rejected_tokens[\"prompt_input_ids\"])\n            prompt_len_input_ids = min(chosen_prompt_len_input_ids, rejected_prompt_len_input_ids)\n\n            for k, v in prompt_tokens.items():\n                prompt_tokens[k] = v[:prompt_len_input_ids]\n\n            # Make sure prompts only have one different token at most an\n            # and length only differs by 1 at most\n            num_diff_tokens = sum(\n                [a != b for a, b in zip(chosen_tokens[\"prompt_input_ids\"], rejected_tokens[\"prompt_input_ids\"])]\n            )\n            num_diff_len = abs(chosen_prompt_len_input_ids - rejected_prompt_len_input_ids)\n            if num_diff_tokens > 1 or num_diff_len > 1:\n                raise ValueError(\n                    \"Chosen and rejected prompt_input_ids might only differ on the \"\n                    \"last token due to tokenizer merge ops.\"\n                )\n\n            # add BOS token to head of prompt. Avoid adding if it's already there\n            bos_token_id = self.tokenizer.bos_token_id\n            if prompt_len_input_ids == 0 or bos_token_id != prompt_tokens[\"prompt_input_ids\"][0]:\n                prompt_tokens[\"prompt_input_ids\"] = [bos_token_id] + prompt_tokens[\"prompt_input_ids\"]\n                prompt_tokens[\"prompt_attention_mask\"] = [1] + prompt_tokens[\"prompt_attention_mask\"]\n            if chosen_prompt_len_input_ids == 0 or bos_token_id != chosen_tokens[\"prompt_input_ids\"][0]:\n                chosen_tokens[\"prompt_input_ids\"] = [bos_token_id] + chosen_tokens[\"prompt_input_ids\"]\n                chosen_tokens[\"prompt_attention_mask\"] = [1] + chosen_tokens[\"prompt_attention_mask\"]\n            if rejected_prompt_len_input_ids == 0 or bos_token_id != rejected_tokens[\"prompt_input_ids\"][0]:\n                rejected_tokens[\"prompt_input_ids\"] = [bos_token_id] + rejected_tokens[\"prompt_input_ids\"]\n                rejected_tokens[\"prompt_attention_mask\"] = [1] + rejected_tokens[\"prompt_attention_mask\"]\n\n            # add EOS token to end of answer. longer_response_length = max(len(chosen_tokens[\"input_ids\"]), len(rejected_tokens[\"input_ids\"]))\n\n            # if combined sequence is too long, truncate the prompt\n            for answer_tokens in [chosen_tokens, rejected_tokens, prompt_tokens]:\n                if len(answer_tokens[\"prompt_input_ids\"]) + longer_response_length > self.max_length:\n                    if self.truncation_mode == \"keep_start\":\n                        for k in [\"prompt_input_ids\", \"prompt_attention_mask\"]:\n                            answer_tokens[k] = answer_tokens[k][: self.max_prompt_length]\n                    elif self.truncation_mode == \"keep_end\":\n                        for k in [\"prompt_input_ids\", \"prompt_attention_mask\"]:\n  # if that's still too long, truncate the response\n            for answer_tokens in [chosen_tokens, rejected_tokens]:\n                if len(answer_tokens[\"prompt_input_ids\"]) + longer_response_length > self.max_length:\n                    for k in [\"input_ids\", \"attention_mask\"]:\n                        answer_tokens[k] = answer_tokens[k][: self.max_length - self.max_prompt_length]\n\n            # Create labels\n            chosen_sequence_tokens = {\n                k: chosen_tokens[f\"prompt_{k}\"] + chosen_tokens[k] for k in [\"input_ids\", \"attention_mask\"]\n            }\n            rejected_sequence_tokens = {\n                k: rejected_tokens[f\"prompt_{k}\"] + rejected_tokens[k] for k in [\"input_ids\", \"attention_mask\"]\n            }\n            chosen_sequence_tokens[\"labels\"] = chosen_sequence_tokens[\"input_ids\"][:]\n            chosen_sequence_tokens[\"labels\"][: len(chosen_tokens[\"prompt_input_ids\"])] = [\n                self.label_pad_token_id\n            ] * len(chosen_tokens[\"prompt_input_ids\"])\n            rejected_sequence_tokens[\"labels\"] = rejected_sequence_tokens[\"input_ids\"][:]\n            rejected_sequence_tokens[\"labels\"][: len(rejected_tokens[\"prompt_input_ids\"])] = [\n                self.label_pad_token_id\n            ] * len(rejected_tokens[\"prompt_input_ids\"])\n\n            for k, toks in {\n                \"chosen_\": chosen_sequence_tokens,\n                \"rejected_\": rejected_sequence_tokens,\n                \"\": prompt_tokens,\n            }.items():\n                for type_key, tokens in toks.items():\n                    if type_key == \"token_type_ids\":\n                        continue\n                    batch[f\"{k}{type_key}\"] = tokens\n\n        else:\n            chosen_tokens = self.tokenizer(\n                chosen, truncation=True, max_length=self.max_target_length, add_special_tokens=True\n            )\n            rejected_tokens = self.tokenizer(\n                rejected, truncation=True, max_length=self.max_target_length, add_special_tokens=True\n            )\n            prompt_tokens = self.tokenizer(\n                prompt, truncation=True, max_length=self.max_prompt_length, add_special_tokens=True\n            )\n\n            batch[\"chosen_labels\"] = chosen_tokens[\"input_ids\"]\n            batch[\"rejected_labels\"] = rejected_tokens[\"input_ids\"]\n            batch[\"prompt_input_ids\"] = prompt_tokens[\"input_ids\"]\n            batch[\"prompt_attention_mask\"] = prompt_tokens[\"attention_mask\"]\n\n            if model is not None and hasattr(model, \"prepare_decoder_input_ids_from_labels\"):\n                batch[\"rejected_decoder_input_ids\"] = model.prepare_decoder_input_ids_from_labels(\n                    labels=torch.tensor(batch[\"chosen_labels\"])\n                )\n\n        return batch\n\n    @staticmethod\n    def concatenated_inputs(\n        batch: Dict[str, Union[List, torch.LongTensor]],\n        is_encoder_decoder: bool = False,\n        label_pad_token_id: int = -100,\n        padding_value: int = 0,\n        device: Optional[torch.device] = None,\n    ) -> Dict[str, torch.LongTensor]:\n        \"\"\"Concatenate the chosen and rejected inputs into a single tensor.\n\n        Args:\n            batch: A batch of data. Must contain the keys 'chosen_input_ids' and 'rejected_input_ids', which are tensors of shape (batch_size, sequence_length).\n            is_encoder_decoder: Whether the model is an encoder-decoder model.\n            label_pad_token_id: The label pad token id.\n            padding_value: The padding value to use for the concatenated inputs_ids.\n            device: The device for the concatenated inputs.\n\n        Returns:\n            A dictionary containing the concatenated inputs under the key 'concatenated_input_ids'.\n        \"\"\"\n        concatenated_batch = {}\n\n max(batch[\"chosen_input_ids\"].shape[1], batch[\"rejected_input_ids\"].shape[1])\n\n        for k in batch:\n            if k.startswith(\"chosen\") and isinstance(batch[k], torch.Tensor):\n                if \"labels\" in k or is_encoder_decoder:\n                    pad_value = label_pad_token_id\n                elif k.endswith(\"_input_ids\"):\n                    pad_value = padding_value\n                elif k.endswith(\"_attention_mask\"):\n                    pad_value = 0\n                concatenated_key = k.replace(\"chosen\", \"concatenated\")\n                concatenated_batch[concatenated_key] = pad_to_length(batch[k], max_length, pad_value=pad_value)\n        for k in batch:\n            if k.startswith(\"rejected\") and isinstance(batch[k], torch.Tensor):\n                if \"labels\" in k or is_encoder_decoder:\n                    pad_value = label_pad_token_id\n                elif k.endswith(\"_input_ids\"):\n                    pad_value = padding_value\n                elif k.endswith(\"_attention_mask\"):\n                    pad_value = 0\n                concatenated_key = k.replace(\"rejected\", \"concatenated\")\n                concatenated_batch[concatenated_key] = torch.cat(\n                    (\n                        concatenated_batch[concatenated_key],\n                        pad_to_length(batch[k], max_length, pad_value=pad_value),\n                    ),\n                    dim=0,\n                ).to(device=device)\n\n        if is_encoder_decoder:\n            concatenated_batch[\"concatenated_input_ids\"] = batch[\"prompt_input_ids\"].repeat(2, 1).to(device=device)\n            concatenated_batch[\"concatenated_attention_mask\"] = (\n                batch[\"prompt_attention_mask\"].repeat(2, 1).to(device=device)\n            )\n\n        return concatenated_batch\n\n    def simpo_loss(\n        self,\n        policy_chosen_logps: torch.FloatTensor,\n        policy_rejected_logps: torch.FloatTensor,\n    ) -> Tuple[torch.FloatTensor, torch.FloatTensor, torch.FloatTensor]:\n        \"\"\"Compute the SimPO loss for a batch of policy model log probabilities.\n\n        Args:\n            policy_chosen_logps: Log probabilities of the policy model for the chosen responses. Shape: (batch_size,)\n            policy_rejected_logps: Log probabilities of the policy model for the rejected responses. Shape: (batch_size,)\n\n        Returns:\n            A tuple of three tensors: (losses, chosen_rewards, rejected_rewards).\n            The losses tensor contains the SimPO loss for each example in the batch.\n            The chosen_rewards and rejected_rewards tensors contain the rewards for the chosen and rejected responses, respectively.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        return losses, chosen_rewards, rejected_rewards\n\n    def concatenated_forward(\n        self, model: nn.Module, batch: Dict[str, Union[List, torch.LongTensor]]\n    ) -> Tuple[torch.FloatTensor, torch.FloatTensor, torch.FloatTensor, torch.FloatTensor]:\n        \"\"\"Run the given model on the given batch of inputs, concatenating the chosen and rejected inputs together.\n\n        We do this to avoid doing two forward passes, because it's faster for FSDP.\n        \"\"\"\n        concatenated_batch = self.concatenated_inputs(\n            batch,\n            is_encoder_decoder=self.is_encoder_decoder,\n            label_pad_token_id=self.label_pad_token_id,\n            padding_value=self.padding_value,\n            device=self.accelerator.device,\n        )\n        len_chosen = concatenated_batch.pop(\"concatenated_decoder_input_ids\", None),\n            }\n            if self.is_encoder_decoder\n            else {}\n        )\n\n        all_logits = model(\n            concatenated_batch[\"concatenated_input_ids\"],\n            attention_mask=concatenated_batch[\"concatenated_attention_mask\"],\n            use_cache=False,\n            **model_kwargs,\n concatenated_batch[\"concatenated_labels\"][:len_chosen]\n\n        return (chosen_logps, rejected_logps, chosen_logits, rejected_logits, chosen_labels)\n\n    @staticmethod\n    def get_batch_logps(\n        logits: torch.FloatTensor,\n        labels: torch.LongTensor,\n        average_log_prob: bool = True,\n        label_pad_token_id: int = -100,\n        is_encoder_decoder: bool = False,\n    ) -> torch.FloatTensor:\n        \"\"\"Compute the log probabilities of the given labels under the given logits.\n\n        Args:\n            logits: Logits of the model (unnormalized). Shape: (batch_size, sequence_length, vocab_size)\n            labels: Labels for which to compute the log probabilities. Label tokens with a value of label_pad_token_id are ignored. Shape: (batch_size, sequence_length)\n            average_log_prob: If True, return the average log probability per (non-masked) token. Otherwise, return the sum of the log probabilities of the (non-masked) tokens.\n            label_pad_token_id: The label pad token id.\n            is_encoder_decoder: Whether the model is an encoder-decoder model.\n\n        Returns:\n            A tensor of shape (batch_size,) containing the average/sum log probabilities of the given labels under the given logits.\n        \"\"\"\n labels = labels[:, 1:].clone()\n            logits = logits[:, :-1, :]\n        loss_mask = labels != label_pad_token_id\n\n        # dummy token; we'll ignore the losses on these tokens later\n return (per_token_logps * loss_mask).sum(-1) / loss_mask.sum(-1)\n        else:\n            return (per_token_logps * loss_mask).sum(-1)\n\n    def get_batch_loss_metrics(\n        self,\n        model,\n        batch: Dict[str, Union[List, torch.LongTensor]],\n        train_eval: Literal[\"train\", \"eval\"] = \"train\",\n    ):\n        \"\"\"Compute the SimPO loss and other metrics for the given batch of inputs for train or test.\"\"\"\n        metrics = {}\n        prefix = \"eval_\" if train_eval == \"eval\" else \"\"\n\n        (\n            policy_chosen_logps,\n self.sft_weight * sft_loss + loss\n            metrics[f\"{prefix}sft_loss\"] = sft_loss.detach().cpu()\n        \n        reward_accuracies = (chosen_rewards > rejected_rewards).float()\n\n        metrics[f\"{prefix}rewards/chosen\"] = chosen_rewards.mean().cpu()\n        metrics[f\"{prefix}rewards/rejected\"] = rejected_rewards.mean().cpu()\n        metrics[f\"{prefix}rewards/accuracies\"] = reward_accuracies.mean().cpu()\n        metrics[f\"{prefix}rewards/margins\"] = (chosen_rewards - rejected_rewards).mean().cpu()\n        metrics[f\"{prefix}logps/rejected\"] = policy_rejected_logps.detach().mean().cpu()\n        metrics[f\"{prefix}logps/chosen\"] = policy_chosen_logps.detach().mean().cpu()\n        metrics[f\"{prefix}logits/rejected\"] = policy_rejected_logits.detach().mean().cpu()\n        metrics[f\"{prefix}logits/chosen\"] = policy_chosen_logits.detach().mean().cpu()\n\n        return loss, metrics\n\n    def compute_loss(\n        self,\n        model: Union[PreTrainedModel, nn.Module],\n        inputs: Dict[str, Union[torch.Tensor, Any]],\n        return_outputs=False,\n    ) -> Union[torch.Tensor, Tuple[torch.Tensor,
